                                Figure 4-9. SBCT Example of a reverse slope

         Note. FIGURE 4-7 SCENARIO — The troop commander places the FO team and MGS platoon,
         forward to engage. The forward Infantry platoon splits into sections, dismount, and secure the high
         ground on the flanks of the MGS platoon. They engage the enemy in EA Alpha to draw them in.
         On order, the forward element disengages and repositions. The Stryker Infantry rifle platoon
         consolidates in its secondary position. The MGS platoon moves to a reserve or striker force behind
         the second hill. The company engages the remaining enemy force in EAs Echo, Omega, and Zulu
         in a reverse slope defense. (See figure 4-10, page 4-40.)

4-38       ATP 3-21.91  11 May 2017


---

                                                                                                                                           Defense

ASSESS

     4-181. Assessment is continuous throughout the completion of prepare and execution phases. Identification
     of CCIRs assists the assessments with the decision support templates or the commanders’ orders. Assessment
     will force the commander to determine commitment of the reserve force, engagement/break contact,
     consolidate and reorganize, counterattack, resupply or recommend pursuit.

  SECTION VII – ENGAGEMENT AREA DEVELOPMENT

     4-182. An engagement area is an area where the commander intends to contain and destroy an enemy force
     with the massed effects of all available weapons and supporting systems (FM 3-90-1). By studying the terrain,
     the commander tries to determine the principal enemy and friendly heavy, light, and air avenues of approach.
     The commander determines the most advantageous area for the enemy’s main attack, using the five military
     aspects of terrain OAKOC. (See ATP 3-34.80 for a detailed discussion of OAKOC.)

     4-183. The success of any engagement depends on how effectively the commander can integrate the
     obstacle plan, the indirect fire plan, and the direct fire plan within the EA to achieve the SBCT weapons troop
     tactical purpose. (See chapter 9 of this publication for more information). For the SBCT weapons troop and
     its platoon’s integrations with other forces such as Cavalry and Infantry enhance the lethality of the
     engagement area and the effectiveness to combat the enemy combined Arms formation.

     4-184. At the company level, EA development is a complex function, demanding parallel planning and
     preparation. Despite this complexity, EA development resembles a drill in that the commander and his
     subordinate leaders use an orderly, fairly standard set of procedures. The steps of EA development are not a
     rigid sequential process; some steps may occur simultaneously to ensure the synergy of combined arms. The
     development process covers these steps beginning with evaluation of METT-TC variables:

                z Identify all likely enemy avenues of approach.
                z Determine likely enemy schemes of maneuver.
                z Determine where to kill the enemy.
                z Plan and integrate obstacles.
                z Emplace weapons systems.
                z Plan and integrate indirect fires.
                z Rehearse the execution of operations in the EA.

IDENTIFY LIKELY ENEMY AVENUES OF APPROACH

     4-185. The troop commander and the COIST, if available, can use the following techniques and
     considerations when identifying the enemy’s likely avenues of approach (see figure 4-8, page 4-36):

                z Conduct initial reconnaissance of the terrain using OAKOC. If possible, do this from the
                     enemy’s perspective along each avenue of approach into the sector of fire or EA. JCR/BFT can
                     graphically assist the troop commander by building a modified combined obstacle overlay.

                z Identify key and decisive terrain. This includes locations that afford positions of advantage over
                     the enemy, and natural obstacles and choke points that restrict forward movement.

                z Determine which avenues provide cover and concealment for the enemy while allowing him to
                     maintain his tempo. JCR/BFT can graphically assist the company commander by showing line
                     of sight data.

                z Determine which terrain the enemy is likely to use to support each avenue.
                z Evaluate lateral routes adjoining each avenue of approach.

11 May 2017  ATP 3-21.91  4-39


---

Chapter 4

    Legend: AA = avenue of approach

                                  Figure 4-10. Likely enemy avenues of approach

DETERMINE ENEMY SCHEME OF MANEUVER

     4-186. The SBCT weapons troop commander and COIST, if available, can use the following considerations
     when determining the enemy’s scheme of maneuver (see figure 4-11):

                z Determine how the enemy will structure the attack.
                z Determine how the enemy will use his reconnaissance assets. Will he attempt to infiltrate

                     friendly positions?
                z Determine where and when the enemy will change formations or establish support by fire

                     positions.
                z Determine where, when, and how the enemy will conduct his assault or breaching operations.
                z Determine where and when he will commit follow-on forces.
                z Determine the enemy’s expected rates of movement.
                z Assess the effects of his combat multipliers, the anticipated locations, and areas of employment.
                z Determine what reactions the enemy is likely to have in response to projected friendly actions.

4-40       ATP 3-21.91  11 May 2017


---

                          Defense

    Legend: AA = avenue of approach

                                      Figure 4-11. Enemy scheme of maneuver

DETERMINE WHERE TO ENGAGE THE ENEMY

     4-187. The following steps identify and mark where the SBCT weapons troop will engage the enemy (see
     figure 4-12, page 4-42). This step the commander develops terrain based fire control measures (See chapter 9
     of this publication for more information) :

                z Identify TRPs that match the enemy’s scheme of maneuver allowing the SBCT weapons troop
                     to identify where it will engage enemy forces through the depth of the sector of fire.

                z Identify and record the exact location, composition, and intent of each TRP. TRPs within the
                     SBCT can be of different composition because of the use of different weapon systems to engage
                     different types of targets. For example thermal sights are used to ensure visibility at the
                     appropriate range under varying conditions when marking TRPs from ATGM and MGS.
                     Placing TRPs within the EA with heat inside of metal containers provide thermal signatures for
                     the ATGM and MGS sights.

                z Determine trigger line.
                z Determine how many weapons systems will engage fires on each TRP to achieve the desired

                     end state.

                z Determine engagement lines for all weapons systems.
                z Determine which platoons will mass fires on each TRP.
                z Establish EAs around TRPs.
                z Develop the direct fire planning measures necessary to fire at each TRP.

Legend: AA = avenue of approach, EA = engagement area
                                   Figure 4-12. Where to engage the enemy

4-42  ATP 3-21.91                                                                           11 May 2017

                                                                                                                                           Defense

PLAN AND INTEGRATE OBSTACLES

     4-188. The following steps apply in planning and integrating obstacles in the SBCT weapons troop defense
     (see figure 4-13):

                z Determine the obstacle group’s intent confirming the target, relative location, and effect. Ensure
                     intent supports the task force scheme of maneuver.

                z Identify, site, and mark the obstacles within the obstacle group.
                z Integrate protective obstacle types and locations within SBCT weapons troop defense.
                z Ensure coverage of all obstacles with direct fires.
                z Assign responsibility for guides and lane closure, as required.
                z Emplace obstacles based on analysis of the mission variables of METT-TC, secure class IV and

                     V point, secure mine dump, or secure obstacle work sites.
                z Coordinate engineer disengagement criteria, obstacle handover, actions on contact, and security

                     requirements with the engineer platoon leader at the obstacle work site if the company is
                     supported by an engineer platoon.

Chapter 4

EMPLACE WEAPONS SYSTEMS

     4-189. The following steps apply when selecting and improving BPs. (see figure 4-14):
                z Select tentative platoon BPs.

             Note. When possible, select these while moving in the EA. Using the enemy’s perspective enables
             the commander to assess survivability of the positions.

                z Conduct a leader’s reconnaissance of the tentative BPs.
                z Drive the EA to confirm that selected positions are tactically advantageous.
                z Confirm and mark the selected BPs.
                z Ensure that BPs do not conflict with those of adjacent units and that they are effectively tied in

                     with adjacent positions.
                z Select primary, alternate, and supplementary fighting positions to achieve the desired effect for

                     each EA.
                z Ensure that platoon leaders, platoon sergeants (PSGs), section leaders, or vehicle commanders

                     position weapons systems so that each EA is effectively covered by the required number of
                     weapons, vehicles, or platoons.
                z Ensure that positions allow vehicle commanders, loaders, or gunners (as applicable for each
                     vehicle or weapons system) to observe the EA and engage enemy forces from the hull down
                     position.
                z Stake vehicle or weapons system positions according to unit SOP so that the vehicle or weapon
                     system positions can be hardened by the construction of fighting or survivability positions.
                z Confirm all vehicle or weapons system positions.

PLAN AND INTEGRATE INDIRECT FIRES

     4-190. The following steps apply in planning and integrating indirect fires (see figure 4-15, page 4-46).
     This step the commander places threat based fire control measures and synchronizes them with FSCMs (see
     chapter 9 of this publication for more information):

                z Determine the purpose of fires.
                z Determine where that purpose will best be achieved.
                z Establish the observation plan with redundancy for each target. Observers will include the FIST

                     and members of maneuver elements with fire support responsibilities.
                z Establish triggers based on enemy movement rates.
                z Obtain accurate target locations using survey or navigational equipment.
                z Refine target locations to ensure coverage of obstacles.
                z Adjust artillery and mortar targets.
                z Plan FPF.
